marking  or  writing  therein  with 
ink  or  pencil,  tearing  or  taking 
out  its  pages  or  otherwise 
damaging  it,  wiii  constitute  an 
injury  to  a book.

KHASI  AUTHORS’  SOCIETY 
(K 1 Seng  Ki  Nongthoh  Kot  Khasi) 


The  6th  Sept,  1980 

In  these  days  when  both  egoism  and  egotism  still 
play  an  important  part  in  the  mind  of  most  Khasi  authors, 
it  is  encouraging  to  note  that  young  authors  like  Hugh- 
let  Wajjri  and  Kitbor  VV.  Nongrum  have  been  able  to  rise 
above  those.  In  the  present  work  of  Nongrum.  Ki 
Nongthoh-Kot  Khasi  which  he  divides  into  two  volumes 
a reader  would  appreciate  the  difficulty  he  encountered 
while  collecting  his  materials,  especially  when  he  deals 
with  authors  of  the  past.  If  works  as  this  one  were  to  be 
undertaken  by  a government  department,  the  expenditure 
to  be  incurred  would  not  be  less  than  Rs.  50.000/-  on 
various  items;  the  present  and  future  generations  of 
Khasi  authors  owe  a debt  to  Nongrum  for  the  present 
work  because  each  chapter  is  enough  basis  for  methodo- 
logical treatment  for  some  higher  degi-ees  than  the  M A. 
and  the  M.  Phil  in  Khasi  literature.  We  would  be  doing 
injustice  to  our  own  literary  heritage  if  work  such  as  this 
one  (which  seeks  to  bind  the  information  into  one,  irres- 
pective of  age.  religion,  and  social  status  of  the  personali- 
ties Nongrum  chooses  for  each  of  the  volumes)  could  not 
be  encouraged.  I for  one  would  like  all  other  readers  to 
have  their  copies  as  soon  as  those  are  available. 


H.  W.  Sten. 
PRESIDENT 

KHASI  AUTHORS’  SOCIETY.

Serampore  ba  la  ialam  da  u Mishoneri  ka  Balang  Baptist.

jabieng,  ka  jingtrei  shitom  bad  Ka  jingdemminot  ka  Jong

a highly  elaborate  one  and  the  contents  rather  difficult  to 
digest.

especially  been  given  to  meet  the  wants  of  Khasi  Students.’

la  ka  jingim  jinglong  bad  la  ka  por  shongskul  ha  ka  ki

Kumta  ka  long  kaba  phylla  ban  tip  ba  u Amjad  AH  u

KA  MYMTOI 
Dykhot  I 

Lane  ka  Kot  Boit  (Poetry)  kaba  Nyngkong 

U la  pynmih  la  kane  ka  kot  na  Laban,  Shillong  ha  ka 
sncm  1888,  bad  u la  phah  shon  ta  ka  ba  ka  Baptist  Mission 
Press,  Calcutta. 

Ha  ka  sla  kaba  nyngkong  jong  kane  ka  kot  ngi  shem 
ia  kine  ki  ktien; 

“Wat  kynnoh  pop  ia  ki  briw 
Lada  phi  shem  ki  jinglait: 

Namar  ym  don  tang  wei  ruh 
Ban  lait  na  ki  jinglait. 

Jing  pynbeit  sngowbha 
A A-” 

Ha  ka  ka  ktien  lamphrang  jong  kane  ka  kot  (20  tank  u 
Risaw,  1888),  u Amjad  Ali  u ong  kumne:  “The  want  of  an 
elementary  poetical  work  in  Khasi  for  the  use  of  Khasi 
Children  has  long  been  telt . 

“There  are,  doubtless,  many  persons  more  qualified 
than  myself  to  supply  the  want,  but  as  none  of  them  have 
come  forward,  I thought  it  best  to  lead  the  way  by  printing 
the  following  few  pages  which  are  the  first  of  a series 
intended  for  the  purpose.” 

’'T  am  conscious  of  the  numerous  faults  and  imperfec- 
tion my  work,  and  am  well  aware  that  I am  not  at  all  disci- 
plined in  the  art  of  authorship.  Still  I hope  that  these  few 
pages  may  be  treated  with  kindness  as  an  ancouragement, 
at  least  to  Khasi  poetry,  and  I may  be  allowed  ample 
quarter  on  account  of  the  poorness  of  the  language.”

Ha  kane  ka  kot,  ki  don  18  tylli  ki  poitri:—  Step; 
Byneng;  U Blei;  kajingstad;  ka  jingim;  ka  jingalhia;  Synshar

u Nongkhaii  bad  u Napit;  ‘Tiew-patliai-khubor,  Jingshad 
Khasi.  Nalor  kine,  ka  don  rub  kawei  ka  jmgthoh — 'shi 
lynnong  ki  fingong'

U bynai  u la  rib.

Khlur  ia  jab  jar.'*

da  kine  ki  ktien:  — 

"Kynmaw  kynmaw  ki  kticnjong  U 
Kynmaw  kynmaw  hukum  jong  U 
To  kbmib  la  ki  jingtrei  rub 
Wat  leit  klet  ia  U sbuh”  (U  Blei)

kumne; — 


“Ko  nong-pulc:  mapbi  barob 
Hikai  bikai,  bikai  jingstad
